2. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $q^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  with a  $k$ -row-sparse  $n \times n$  matrix  $(\gamma^{\mathrm{new}}m^{\mathrm{tmp}})$  (part 1 of fact) takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, k, n))$  time.

so in total computing  $n_{1}$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)},k,n))$  time.

the second part is to compute the  $n \times n$  matrix  $n_2$ . by the definition of  $m^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  we have

$$
n _ {2} = r \sqrt {v} (m ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} - m) = r \sqrt {v} m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \cdot \underbrace {((\delta_ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} , s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}) ^ {- 1} + m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} , s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}) ^ {- 1} (m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}) ^ {\top}} _ {n _ {3}}.
$$

and we computes  $n_{2}$  as follows:

1. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $r$  with a  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\sqrt{v}$  takes  $o(n^{2 + o(1)})$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $r\sqrt{v}$  with a  $n \times o(k)$  matrix  $m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}}$  (part 1 of fact e.11) takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n, k))$  time.  
3. the  $o(k) \times n$  matrix  $n_{3}$  is already computed when we were computing  $m^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  (claim e.13), and this takes  $o(k^{\omega + o(1)} + \mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(k, k, n))$  time.  
4. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times o(k)$  matrix  $(r\sqrt{v} m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}})$  with a  $o(k) \times n$  matrix  $n_3$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, k, n))$  time.

so in total computing  $q^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes time

$$
o (n ^ {2 + o (1)} + k ^ {\omega + o (1)} + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (k, k, n) + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n ^ {1 + o (1)}, k, n)) = o (k ^ {\omega + o (1)} + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n ^ {1 + o (1)}, k, n)).
$$

claim e.15 (part 3 of lemma e.12). in matrixupdate, it takes  $o(n^{2 + o(1)})$  time to compute

$$
\beta_ {1} \leftarrow q ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}).
$$

proof. to compute  $\beta_{1}$ , we first multiply a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $q^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  with a  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}}$ , which takes  $o(n^{2 + o(1)})$  time. then we multiply a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $q^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}}$  with a  $n \times 1$  vector, which takes  $o(n^{2 + o(1)})$  time. so in total computing  $\beta_{1}$  takes time  $o(n^{2 + o(1)})$ .

claim e.16 (part 4 of lemma e.12). in procedure matrixupdate, it takes  $o(n^2)$  time to compute

$$
\beta_ {2} \leftarrow m ^ {\operatorname {t m p}} \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}).
$$

proof. to compute  $\beta_{2}$ , we first multiply a  $n\times n$  matrix  $m^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  with a  $n\times n$  diagonal matrix  $\sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}}$  which takes  $o(n^{2})$  time. then we multiply a  $n^{1}\times n$  matrix  $m^{\mathrm{tmp}}\sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}}$  with a  $n\times 1$  vector, which takes  $o(n^{2})$  time. so in total computing  $\beta_{1}$  takes time  $o(n^{2})$ .

proof of lemma e.12. overall time. the running time of procedure matrixupdate is

$$
o (k ^ {\omega + o (1)} + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n ^ {1 + o (1)}, k, n) + n ^ {2 + o (1)}) = o (\mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n ^ {1 + o (1)}, k, n)).
$$

# e.4 running time of partialmatrixupdate

the goal of this section is to prove lemma e.18. we will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in lemma e.1.

fact e.17 (sparsity guarantees for partialmatrixupdate). when entering partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14) we have the following sparsity guarantee (from table 12 and table 13):

1.  $\| \sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}} -\sqrt{v}\| _0\leq n^a$

4.  $\| f(\widetilde{g}) - f(g)\| _0\leq n^a$

7.  $|\partial s|\leq \widetilde{k}\leq 2n^{a}$

2.  $\| \partial \gamma \| _0\leq \widetilde{k}\leq 2n^a$

5.  $\| \xi \| _0\leq n^a$

3.  $\| \gamma +\partial \gamma \| _0\leq n^a$

6.  $|s|\leq n^a$

lemma e.18 (running time of partialmatrixupdate). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14) it takes

1.  $o(\widetilde{kn}^a)$  time to compute

$$
\left(u ^ {\prime}, c, u\right) \leftarrow \operatorname {d e c o m p o s e} \big (\mathcal {l} _ {*} \big [ \left(\delta_ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}, s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}\right) ^ {- 1} + m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}, s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \big ] - \mathcal {l} _ {*} \big [ \delta_ {s, s} ^ {- 1} + m _ {s, s} \big ] \big),
$$

2.  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^a,n^a))$  time to compute

$$
b ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets b - b u ^ {\prime} (c ^ {- 1} + u ^ {\top} b u ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1} u ^ {\top} b,
$$

3.  $o(n)$  time to compute

$$
\xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} f (\widetilde {g}) - \sqrt {v} f (g),
$$

4.  $o(n^{2a})$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow b ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {r} \left[ \beta_ {2, s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} \right] + b ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {r} \left[ \left(m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}}\right) ^ {\top} \right] \xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}},
$$

5.  $o(\widetilde{kn}^a)$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \gamma_ {2} + (\gamma + \partial \gamma) m (\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} - \sqrt {\widetilde {v}}) f (\widetilde {g}) + \partial \gamma m (\sqrt {\widetilde {v}} f (\widetilde {g}) - \sqrt {v} f (g)).
$$

6.  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)},n^a,\widetilde{k}))$  time to compute

$$
f ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets f + r \gamma \cdot (\mathcal {l} _ {c} [ m _ {\partial s \backslash s} ] - \mathcal {l} _ {c} [ m _ {s ^ {\prime}} ]) + r \partial \gamma \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {c} [ m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} ].
$$

7.  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^a,\widetilde{k}))$  time to compute

$$
e ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} = e + b ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} (\mathcal {l} _ {r} [ (m _ {\partial s \backslash s}) ^ {\top} ] - \mathcal {l} _ {r} [ (m _ {s ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\top} ]) - b u ^ {\prime} (c ^ {- 1} + u ^ {\top} b u ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1} u ^ {\top} e.
$$

further, it takes  $o(n)$  time to do all other computation. so the overall running time of the procedure partialmatrixupdate is  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n^a, \widetilde{k}))$ .

claim e.19 (part 1 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(\widetilde{kn}^a)$  time to compute

$$
(u ^ {\prime}, c, u) \gets \mathrm {d e c o m p o s e} \big (\mathcal {l} _ {*} [ (\delta_ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}, s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}) ^ {- 1} + m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}, s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} ] - \mathcal {l} _ {*} [ \delta_ {s, s} ^ {- 1} + m _ {s, s} ] \big).
$$

and the size of the computed matrices are  $u', u \in \mathbb{r}^{6n^a \times c}$ ,  $c \in \mathbb{r}^{c \times c}$ , where  $c = o(\widetilde{k})$ .

proof. the analysis for the decompose function in partialmatrixupdate is the same as the analysis in query (claim e.7). we still have  $|s| \leq n^a$  (part 6 of fact e.17), but the bound on  $\partial s$  is different and now we have  $|\partial s| = \widetilde{k} \leq 2n^a$  (part 7 of fact e.17). so  $c = o(|\partial s|) = o(\widetilde{k})$ .

and to compute  $(u', c, u)$  it takes  $o(\tilde{kn}^a)$  time.

claim e.20 (part 2 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^a,n^a))$  time to compute

$$
b ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow b - b u ^ {\prime} \underbrace {(c ^ {- 1} + u ^ {\top} b u ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} _ {n} u ^ {\top} b.
$$

proof. the running time of computing  $b^{\mathrm{tmp}} \in \mathbb{r}^{6n^a \times 6n^a}$  can be split into the following parts.

the first part is to compute  $n\in \mathbb{r}^{c\times c}$  as follows:

1. multiplying a  $c \times 6n^a$  matrix  $u^\top$  with a  $6n^a \times 6n^a$  matrix  $b$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c, n^a, n^a))$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $c \times 6n^a$  matrix  $(u^\top b)$  with a  $6n^a \times c$  matrix  $u'$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c, n^a, c))$  time.  
3. computing the inverse of a  $c \times c$  matrix  $(c^{-1} + u^{\top}bu')$  takes  $o(c^{\omega + o(1)})$  time.

so the total running time to compute  $n$  is  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^a,n^a) + \mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^a,c) + c^{\omega +o(1)}) = o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k},n^a,n^a))$  (since  $c = o(\widetilde{k})\leq o(n^{a})$  by claim e.19), and  $\widetilde{k}\leq 2n^a$

the second part is to compute  $bu'nu^\top b \in \mathbb{r}^{6n^a \times 6n^a}$  as follows:

1. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times 6n^{a}$  matrix  $b$  with a  $6n^{a} \times c$  matrix  $u'$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a}, n^{a}, c))$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $c \times 6n^a$  matrix  $u^\top$  with a  $6n^a \times 6n^a$  matrix  $b$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c, n^a, n^a))$  time.  
3. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times c$  matrix  $bu'$  with a  $c \times c$  matrix  $n$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^a, c, c))$  time.  
4. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times c$  matrix  $bu'n$  with a  $c \times 6n^{a}$  matrix  $u^{\top}b$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,n^{a}))$  time.

so the total running time to compute  $bu'nu^\top b$  is  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^a, n^a, c) + \mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^a, c, c)) = o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^a, n^a, \widetilde{k}))$  (since  $c = o(\widetilde{k}) \leq o(n^a)$  by claim e.19), and  $\widetilde{k} \leq 2n^a$ .

finally, subtracting  $bu'nu^\top b$  from  $b$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time since both matrices have size  $6n^a \times 6n^a$ . so in total computing  $b^{\mathrm{tmp}} \in \mathbb{r}^{6n^a \times 6n^a}$  takes time  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k}, n^a, n^a) + n^{2a}) = o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k}, n^a, n^a))$ .

□

claim e.21 (part 3 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(n)$  time to compute  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets \sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}} f(\widetilde{g}) - \sqrt{v} f(g)$ . and the computed vector  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times 1}$  is  $n^a$ -sparse.

proof. the  $o(n)$  running time follows trivially from the fact that  $\sqrt{w^{\mathrm{appr}}}$  and  $\sqrt{v}$  are  $n\times n$  diagonal matrices, and  $f(\widetilde{g})$  and  $f(g)$  are  $n\times 1$  vectors. we also have

$$
\| \xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \| _ {0} = \| \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} f (\widetilde {g}) - \sqrt {v} f (g) \| _ {0} = \max \{\| \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} - \sqrt {v} \| _ {0}, \| f (\widetilde {g}) - f (g) \| _ {0} \} \leq n ^ {a},
$$

which follows from part 1 and 4 of fact e.17.

claim e.22 (part 4 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time to compute  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets b^{\mathrm{tmp}} \cdot \mathcal{l}_r[\beta_{2,s^{\mathrm{new}}}] + b^{\mathrm{tmp}} \cdot \mathcal{l}_r[(m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}})^\top] \cdot \xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$ .

proof. the running time of computing  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

1. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times 6n^{a}$  matrix  $b^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  with a  $6n^{a} \times 1$  vector  $\mathcal{l}_r[\beta_{2,s^{\mathrm{new}}}]$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times n$  matrix  $\mathcal{l}_r[(m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}})^\top]$  with a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  (claim e.21) takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.  
3. multiplying a  $6n^a \times 6n^a$  matrix  $b^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  with a  $6n^a \times 1$  vector  $\mathcal{l}_r[(m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}})^\top]\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.

so in total computing  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.

claim e.23 (part 5 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(\widetilde{kn}^a)$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow \gamma_ {2} + (\gamma + \partial \gamma) m \underbrace {(\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} - \sqrt {\widetilde {v}}) f (\widetilde {g})} _ {a _ {1}} + \partial \gamma m \underbrace {(\sqrt {\widetilde {v}} f (\widetilde {g}) - \sqrt {v} f (g))} _ {a _ {2}}.
$$

proof. first note that the  $n \times 1$  vectors  $a_1$  and  $a_2$  can both be computed in  $o(n)$  time. also,

$$
\| a _ {1} \| _ {0} = \| (\sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} - \sqrt {\widetilde {v}}) f (\widetilde {g}) \| _ {0} \leq \min \{\| \sqrt {w ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}} - \sqrt {\widetilde {v}} \| _ {0}, \| f (\widetilde {g}) \| _ {0} \} \leq \widetilde {k},
$$

where the last step follows from part 1 of fact e.17. and

$$
\| a _ {2} \| _ {0} = \| \sqrt {\widetilde {v}} f (\widetilde {g}) - \sqrt {v} f (g) \| _ {0} = \| \xi \| _ {0} \leq n ^ {a},
$$

where the last step follows from part 5 of fact e.17.

the running time of computing  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

1. multiplying a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $(\gamma + \partial \gamma)$  (part 3 of fact e.17) with a  $n \times n$  matrix  $m$  and then with a  $\widetilde{k}$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $a_1$  takes  $o(\widetilde{kn}^a)$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $\widetilde{k}$ -sparse  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\partial \gamma$  (part 2 of fact e.17) with a  $n \times n$  matrix  $m$  and then with a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $a_2$  takes  $o(\widetilde{kn}^a)$  time.

so in total computing  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes time  $o(\widetilde{k} n^a)$ .

claim e.24 (part 6 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n^a, \widetilde{k}))$  time to compute

$$
f ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets f + r \gamma \cdot (\mathcal {l} _ {c} [ m _ {\partial s \backslash s} ] - \mathcal {l} _ {c} [ m _ {s ^ {\prime}} ]) + r \partial \gamma \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {c} [ m _ {s ^ {\mathrm {n e w}}} ].
$$

proof. by sparsity guarantee (part 7, 6 of fact e.17), we have  $|\partial s| + |s'| \leq |\partial s| \leq \widetilde{k} \leq 2n^a$ . the running time of computing  $f^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

1. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $r$  with a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\gamma$  and then with a  $\widetilde{k}$ -column-sparse  $n \times 6n^a$  matrix  $\mathcal{l}_c[m_{\partial s \setminus s}] - \mathcal{l}_c[m_{s'}]$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n^a, \widetilde{k}))$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $r$  with a  $\widetilde{k}$ -sparse  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\partial \gamma$  and then with a  $n \times 6n^a$  matrix  $\mathcal{l}_c[m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}}]$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)},\widetilde{k},n^a)$  time.  
3. adding two matrices  $r\gamma(\mathcal{l}_c[m_{\partial s \setminus s}] - \mathcal{l}_c[m_{s'}])$ ,  $r\partial \gamma \mathcal{l}_c[m_{s^{\mathrm{new}}}]$  on the current stored matrix  $f$  takes  $o(n^{1 + o(1) + a})$  time.

so in total computing  $f^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n^a, \widetilde{k}) + n^{1 + o(1) + a}) = o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n^a, \widetilde{k}))$  time since  $o(n^{1 + o(1) + a}) \leq o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{1 + o(1)}, n^a, \widetilde{k}))$ .

claim e.25 (part 7 of lemma e.18). in the procedure partialmatrixupdate (algorithm 14), it takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n^a,\widetilde{k}))$  time to compute

$$
e ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow e + \underbrace {b ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} (\mathcal {l} _ {r} [ (m _ {\partial s \backslash s}) ^ {\top} ] - \mathcal {l} _ {r} [ (m _ {s ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\top} ])} _ {n _ {2}} - \underbrace {b u ^ {\prime} \underbrace {(c ^ {- 1} + u ^ {\top} b u ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} _ {n} u ^ {\top} e} _ {n _ {1}}
$$

proof. by sparsity guarantee (part 7, 6 of fact e.17), we have  $|\partial s| \leq \widetilde{k} \leq 2n^a$ ,  $|s| \leq n^a$ . so  $|s^{\mathrm{new}}| \leq |s \cup \partial s| \leq |s| + |\partial s| \leq 3n^a$ . the running time of computing  $f^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

first we compute  $n_{1}\in \mathbb{r}^{6n^{a}\times n}$

1. we already compute the time of computing  $n$  in claim e.20. it takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c,n^a,n^a))$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times 6n^{a}$  matrix  $b$  with a  $6n^{a} \times c$  matrix  $u'$  takes  $o\big(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a}, n^{a}, c)\big)$  time.  
3. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times c$  matrix  $bu'$  with a  $c \times c$  matrix  $n$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^a, c, c))$  time.  
4. multiplying a  $c \times 6n^a$  matrix  $u^\top$  with a  $6n^a \times n$  matrix  $e$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(c, n^a, n))$  time.  
5. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times c$  matrix  $bu'n$  with a  $c \times n$  matrix  $u^{\top}e$  takes time  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^{a},c,n))$ .

next, we compute  $n_2 \in \mathbb{r}^{6n^a \times n}$ . multiplying a  $6n^a \times 6n^a$  matrix  $b^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  with a  $\widetilde{k}$ -row-sparse  $6n^a \times n$  matrix  $(\mathcal{l}_r[(m_{\partial s \setminus s})^\top] - \mathcal{l}_r[(m_{s'})^\top])$  takes  $o(\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n^a, \widetilde{k}, n))$  time.

so in total computing  $e^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes time

$$
\begin{array}{l} o \left(\mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} \left(c, n ^ {a}, n ^ {a}\right) + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} \left(n ^ {a}, n ^ {a}, c\right) + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} \left(n ^ {a}, c, c\right) + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} \left(c, n ^ {a}, n\right) + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} \left(n ^ {a}, c, n\right)\right) \\ = o \left(\mathcal {t} _ {\operatorname {m a t}} \left(n, n ^ {a}, c\right)\right) = o \left(\mathcal {t} _ {\operatorname {m a t}} \left(n, n ^ {a}, \widetilde {k}\right)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows since  $c = o(\widetilde{k}) \leq o(n^a)$  by claim e.19), and  $\widetilde{k} \leq 2n^a$ .

proof of lemma e.18. overall time. so in total the running time of procedure partialmatrix-update (algorithm 14) is

$$
o (\widetilde {k} n ^ {a} + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (\widetilde {k}, n ^ {a}, n ^ {a}) + n + n ^ {2 a} + \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n, n ^ {a}, \widetilde {k})) = o (\mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n, n ^ {a}, \widetilde {k})).
$$

# e.5 running time of vectorupdate

the goal of this section is to prove lemma e.27. we will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in lemma e.1.

fact e.26 (sparsity guarantees for vectorupdate). when entering vectorupdate (algo-rithm 15) we have the following sparsity guarantee (from table 12 and table 13):

1.  $\| \gamma \| _0 = \| \sqrt{\widetilde{v}} -\sqrt{v}\| _0\leq n^a$

2.  $\| f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(g)\| _0 = p$

lemma e.27 (running time of vectorupdate). the procedure vectorupdate (algorithm 15) takes

1.  $o(pn^{1 + o(1)})$  time to compute

$$
\beta_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow \beta_ {1} + q \sqrt {v} (f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (g)), \quad \beta_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow \beta_ {2} + m \sqrt {v} (f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (g)),
$$

2.  $o(n)$  time to compute

$$
\xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets (\sqrt {\widetilde {v}} - \sqrt {v}) f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}),
$$

3.  $o(n^{2a})$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow b \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {r} \left[ \beta_ {2, s} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \right] + b \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {r} \left[ \left(m _ {s}\right) ^ {\top} \right] \cdot \xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}},
$$

4.  $o(n^{2a})$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \leftarrow \gamma m \cdot \xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}}.
$$

overall, the running time of procedure vectorupdate is

$$
o \left(n ^ {2 a} + p n ^ {1 + o (1)}\right).
$$

claim e.28 (part 1 of lemma e.27). in the procedure vectorupdate (algorithm 15), it takes  $o(pn^{1 + o(1)})$  time to compute

$$
\beta_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \beta_ {1} + q \sqrt {v} (f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (g)), \quad \beta_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \beta_ {2} + m \sqrt {v} (f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (g)).
$$

proof. we compute  $\beta_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  as follows.

1. multiplying a  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\sqrt{v}$  with a  $p$ -sparse vector  $f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(g)$  (part 2 of fact e.26) takes  $o(p)$  time, and the resulting vector  $\sqrt{v}(f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(g))$  is also  $p$ -sparse.  
2. multiplying a  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix  $q$  with a  $p$ -sparse vector  $\sqrt{v} (f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(g))$  takes  $o(pn^{1 + o(1)})$  time.

so the total running time to compute  $\beta_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is  $o(pn^{1 + o(1)})$ .

following the same reason and note that  $m$  has size  $n \times n$ , we can compute  $\beta_2^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  in  $o(pn) \leq o(pn^{1 + o(1)})$  time.

claim e.29 (part 2 of lemma e.27). in the procedure vectorupdate (algorithm 15), it takes  $o(n)$  time to compute  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets (\sqrt{\widetilde{v}} - \sqrt{v}) f(h^{\mathrm{appr}})$ . and the computed  $n \times 1$  vector  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is  $n^a$ -sparse.

proof.  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is computed by multiplying a  $n\times n$  diagonal matrix  $(\sqrt{\widetilde{v}} -\sqrt{v})$  with a  $n\times 1$  vector  $f(h^{\mathrm{app}})$ , and this takes  $o(n)$  time. we also have

$$
\| \xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \| _ {0} = \| (\sqrt {\widetilde {v}} - \sqrt {v}) f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) \| _ {0} \leq \min  \{\| \sqrt {\widetilde {v}} - \sqrt {v} \| _ {0}, \| f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) \| _ {0} \} \leq n ^ {a},
$$

where the last step follows from part 1 of fact e.26.

claim e.30 (part 3 of lemma e.27). in the procedure vectorupdate (algorithm 15), it takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time to compute  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}\gets b\cdot \mathcal{l}_r[\beta_{2,s}^{\mathrm{tmp}}] + b\cdot \mathcal{l}_r[(m_s)^\top ]\cdot \xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$ .

proof. the running time of computing  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

1. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times 6n^{a}$  matrix  $b$  with a  $6n^{a} \times 1$  vector  $\mathcal{l}_r[\beta_{2,s}^{\mathrm{tmp}}]$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.  
2. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times n$  matrix  $\mathcal{l}_r[(m_s)^\top]$  with a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  (claim e.29) takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.  
3. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times 6n^{a}$  matrix  $b$  with a  $6n^{a} \times 1$  vector  $\mathcal{l}_r[(m_s)^\top] \xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.

the total running time to compute  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is  $o(n^{2a})$ .

claim e.31 (part 4 of lemma e.27). in the procedure vectorupdate (algorithm 15), it takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time to compute  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets \gamma m \cdot \xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$ .

proof. we compute  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}} \in \mathbb{r}^n$  by multiplying a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\gamma$  (part 1 of fact e.26) with a  $n \times n$  matrix  $m$  and then with a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  ((claim e.29)), which takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.

proof of lemma e.27. overall, the total running time to compute  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is  $o(pn^{1 + o(1)} + n^{2a}) = o(pn^{1 + o(1)})$ , since we always have  $p \geq n^a$ .

# e.6 running time of partial vector update

the goal of this section is to prove lemma e.33. we will use the following sparsity guarantees that are proved in lemma e.1.

fact e.32 (sparsity guarantees for partial vector update). when entering partial vector update (algorithm 16) we have the following sparsity guarantee (from table 12 and table 13):

$$
1. \| \gamma \| _ {0} \leq n ^ {a},
$$

$$
2. \left\| f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (\widetilde {g}) \right\| _ {0} = \widetilde {p} \leq 2 n ^ {a}.
$$

lemma e.33 (running time of partial vector update). in the procedure partial vector update (in algorithm 16) it takes

1.  $o(n)$  time to compute

$$
\xi^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \sqrt {\widetilde {v}} f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - \sqrt {v} f (g),
$$

2.  $o(n^{2a} + \widetilde{p} n^a)$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {1} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \gamma_ {1} + b \cdot \mathcal {l} _ {r} [ (m _ {s}) ^ {\top} ] \cdot \sqrt {\widetilde {v}} \big (f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (\widetilde {g}) \big),
$$

3.  $o(\widetilde{pn}^a)$  time to compute

$$
\gamma_ {2} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}} \gets \gamma_ {2} + \gamma m \sqrt {\widetilde {v}} \big (f (h ^ {\mathrm {a p p r}}) - f (\widetilde {g}) \big).
$$

overall, the procedure partial vector update takes  $o(n^{2a} + \widetilde{p} n^a)$  time.

claim e.34 (part 1 of lemma e.33). in the procedure partial vector update (algorithm 16), it takes  $o(n)$  time to compute  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets \sqrt{\widetilde{v}} f(h^{\mathrm{appr}}) - \sqrt{v} f(g)$ .

proof.  $\xi^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is computed by multiplying the  $n\times n$  diagonal matrices  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}$  and  $\sqrt{v}$  with the  $n\times 1$  vectors  $f(h^{\mathrm{app}})$  and  $f(g)$  respectively, and this takes  $o(n)$  time.

claim e.35 (part 2 of lemma e.33). in the procedure partialvectorupdate (algorithm 16), it takes  $o(n^{2a} + \widetilde{p} n^a)$  time to compute  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets \gamma_1 + b \cdot \mathcal{l}_r[(m_s)^\top] \cdot \sqrt{\widetilde{v}}\big(f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(\widetilde{g})\big)$ .

proof. the running time of computing  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

1. multiplying a  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}$  with a  $\widetilde{p}$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $(f(h^{\mathrm{appr}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  (part 2 of fact e.32) takes  $o(\widetilde{p})$  time, and the resulting vector  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}(f(h^{\mathrm{appr}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  is also  $\widetilde{p}$ -sparse.  
2. multiplying a  $6n^{a} \times n$  matrix  $\mathcal{l}_r[(m_s)^\top]$  with a  $\widetilde{p}$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}(f(h^{\mathrm{appr}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  takes  $o(\widetilde{p}n^a)$  time.  
3. multiplying a  $6n^a \times 6n^a$  matrix  $b$  with a  $6n^a \times 1$  vector  $\mathcal{l}_r[(m_s)^\top] \sqrt{\widetilde{v}} (f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  takes  $o(n^{2a})$  time.  
4. adding two  $n \times 1$  vectors together takes  $o(n)$  time.

so in total the running time to compute  $\gamma_1^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is  $o(n^{2a} + \widetilde{p} n^a)$ .

claim e.36 (part 3 of lemma e.33). in the procedure partialvectorupdate (algorithm 16), it takes  $o(\widetilde{p} n^a)$  time to compute  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}} \gets \gamma_2 + \gamma m\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}\big(f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(\widetilde{g})\big)$ .

proof. the running time of computing  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  can be split into the following parts:

1. multiplying a  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}$  with a  $\widetilde{p}$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $(f(h^{\mathrm{appr}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  (part 2 of fact e.32) takes  $o(\widetilde{p})$  time, and the resulting vector  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}(f(h^{\mathrm{appr}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  is also  $\widetilde{p}$ -sparse.  
2. multiplying a  $n^a$ -sparse  $n \times n$  diagonal matrix  $\gamma$  (part 1 of fact e.32) with a  $n \times n$  matrix  $m$  and then with a  $\widetilde{p}$ -sparse  $n \times 1$  vector  $\sqrt{\widetilde{v}}(f(h^{\mathrm{app}}) - f(\widetilde{g}))$  takes  $o(\widetilde{p}n^a)$  time.  
3. adding two  $n \times 1$  vectors together takes  $o(n)$  time.

so in total the running time to compute  $\gamma_2^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  is  $o(\widetilde{pn}^a)$ .

proof of lemma e.33. overall, the running time of partial vector update (algorithm 16) is  $o(n^{2a} + \widetilde{p} n^a)$ .

# e.7 running time of initializer

the goal of this section is to prove lemma e.37.

lemma e.37 (running time of initialize). the procedure initialize (in algorithm 5) takes  $o(n^{\omega + o(1)})$  time.

proof. the bottleneck in initialize is to compute  $m = a^{\top}(ava^{\top})^{-1}a$  and  $q = r\sqrt{v}m$ . other operations take at most  $o(n^{2 + o(1)})$  time.

computing  $m$  involves matrix multiplication of two  $n \times n$  matrix, and inversion of a  $n \times n$  matrix. both of them can be done in  $o(n^{\omega + o(1)})$  time.

computing  $q$  involves matrix multiplication of  $n^{1 + o(1)} \times n$  matrix with a  $n \times n$  matrix. this can be done in  $o(n^{\omega + o(1)})$  time.

therefore, the total running time is  $o(n^{\omega + o(1)})$ .

# f data structure : amortized time

in this section we provide an amortized analysis of the four procedures matrixupdate, partial-matrixupdate, vectorupdate, and partialvectorupdate. our amortized analysis builds upon the amortized analysis of [cls19].

# f.1 definitions and preliminaries

our algorithm makes  $t$  calls to the procedure updatequery. for clarity, we define some notations with superscripts that represent the number of iterations. for the input diagonal matrix  $w$  and its approximations  $v$  and  $\widetilde{v}$ , we define the following notations:

definition f.1 (definitions of sequences  $\{w^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$ ,  $\{v^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$  and  $\{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$ ). when initializing, we use  $v^{(0)}$  and  $\widetilde{v}^{(0)}$  to denote the initial values of the data structure members  $v$  and  $\widetilde{v}$ . note that  $v^{(0)} = \widetilde{v}^{(0)} = w^{(0)}$ .

in the  $j$ -th iteration, we use  $w^{(j + 1)}$  to denote the input  $w^{\mathrm{new}}$  of updatequery. since the procedure update might update both  $v$  and  $\widetilde{v}$ , we use  $v^{(j + 1)}$  and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j + 1)}$  to denote the new values of  $v$  and  $\widetilde{v}$  respectively.

we define similar notations for the input query vector  $h$  and its approximations  $g$  and  $\widetilde{g}$ :

definition f.2 (definitions of sequences  $\{h^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$ ,  $\{g^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$  and  $\{\widetilde{g}^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$ ). when initializing, we use  $g^{(0)}$  and  $\widetilde{g}^{(0)}$  to denote the initial values of the data structure members  $g$  and  $\widetilde{g}$ . note that  $g^{(0)} = \widetilde{g}^{(0)} = h^{(0)}$ .

in the  $j$ -th iteration, we use  $h^{(j + 1)}$  to denote the input  $h^{\mathrm{new}}$  of updatequery. since the procedure update might update both  $g$  and  $\widetilde{g}$ , we use  $g^{(j + 1)}$  and  $\widetilde{g}^{(j + 1)}$  to denote the new values of  $g$  and  $\widetilde{g}$  respectively.

the following four notations will be helpful to prove the amortized cost of the procedures:

definition f.3 (definition of  $k$ ,  $\widetilde{k}$ ,  $p$ , and  $\widetilde{p}$ ). in the  $j$ -th iteration, we define the following notations:

1.  $k_{j}$  and  $\widetilde{k}_{j}$  denote outputs  $k$  and  $\widetilde{k}$  of updatev on line 4 of updatequery (algorithm 8).  
2.  $p_j$  and  $\widetilde{p}_j$  denote outputs  $p$  and  $\widetilde{p}$  of updateg on line 5 of updatequery (algorithm 8),

we also define a function  $\psi$  that approximates absolute function  $|x|$  around 0. it will be used to define the potential functions for amortized analysis.

definition f.4 (definition of  $\psi$  function). let  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} \in (0,1/10)$ . let  $\psi: \mathbb{r} \to \mathbb{r}$  be defined by

$$
\psi (x) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {| x | ^ {2}}{2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}, & | x | \in [ 0, \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} ]; \\ \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} - \frac {(2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} - | x |) ^ {2}}{2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}, & | x | \in (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}, 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} ]; \\ \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}, & | x | \in (2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}, + \infty). \end{array} \right.
$$

we also make the following assumption about the values of  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}$  and  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}$ :

assumption f.5. the error parameters satisfy  $0 < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} < 1 / 10$  and  $0 < \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} < \frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}}{100\log n}$ .

# f.2 facts based on adjust and two level of softthreshold

the following facts are direct results from the algorithms, and they are proved solely based on the algorithms. they will be useful when proving the lemmas of amortized running time.

fact f.6 (characterization of  $\widetilde{k}$  (line 4),  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  (line 4),  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}}$  (line 5) of updatev). in the  $j$ -th iteration,  $\forall i \in [n]$ , let  $y_i = |w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1|$ . let  $\pi : [n] \to [n]$  be the sorting such that  $y_{\pi(i)} \geq y_{\pi(i+1)}$ . we have the following guarantees from the description of procedures softthreshold (algorithm 7) and adjust (algorithm 6).

1.  $\{i\in [n]:\psi (w_i^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 2\} \subseteq \pi ([\widetilde{k} ])$  
2.  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{tmp}} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}w_i^{(j + 1)}, & \mathrm{if~}i\in \pi ([\widetilde{k}]);\\ \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}, & \mathrm{otherwise.} \end{array} \right.$  
3.  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} v_i^{(j)}, & \text{if } i \in \pi([\widetilde{k}]) \text{ and } w_i^{(j+1)} \in [(1 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}]; \\ w_i^{(j+1)}, & \text{if } i \in \pi([\widetilde{k}]) \text{ but } w_i^{(j+1)} \notin [(1 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}]; \\ \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}, & \text{otherwise.} \end{array} \right.$  
4.  $\forall i\notin \pi ([\widetilde{k} ])$ $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} - 1| <   \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}$

proof. part 1 and 2. in line 4 in procedure updatev(algorithm 9), we create  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}$ ,  $\widetilde{k}$  by calling procedure softthreshold  $(y_i \gets \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1), w^{(j+1)}, \widetilde{v}^{(j)}, \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 2, n^{\widetilde{a}})$ .

from the initial assignment of  $\widetilde{k}$  on line 5 of softthreshold(algorithm 7), and the fact that the repeat-loop (line 7 to 9) only increases  $k$ , we know that for any  $i$  such that  $y_{i} = \psi((w_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} - \widetilde{v}_{i}) / \widetilde{v}_{i}) \geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 2$ ,  $i \in \pi([\widetilde{k}])$ .

by how we calculate  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}}$  in softthreshold (line 11 of algorithm 7), part 2 follows directly. part 3. we get  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}}$  by calling procedure adjust  $(\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{tmp}},\widetilde{v}^{(j)},v^{(j)},\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})$  (line 5 in procedure updatev, algorithm 9). by part 2 and the rule of how we calculate  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  (see line 5 to 8 in procedure adjust(algorithm 6)):

$$
\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \gets \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {t m p}}
$$

if  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{tmp}}\neq \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$  and  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{tmp}}\in [(1 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v_i^{(j)},(1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v_i^{(j)}]$  then  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}}\gets v_i^{(j)}$

it is easy to see that for  $i \notin \pi([\widetilde{k}])$ ,  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{tmp}} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ , so  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = \widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{tmp}} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ . and for  $i \in \pi([\widetilde{k}])$ , if  $w_i^{(j+1)} \in [(1-\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}, (1+\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}]$ , then  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  is adjusted to be  $v_i^{(j)}$ , otherwise  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = \widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{tmp}} = w_i^{(j+1)}$ .

part 4. from part 3,  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  has three possible values. if  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = w_i^{(j + 1)}$ , we have  $|w_i^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} - 1| = 0$ . if  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = v_i^{(j)}$ , we have  $w_i^{(j + 1)} \in [(1 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}) v_i^{(j)}]$ . so  $|w_i^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} - 1| < \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}$  (assumption f.5).

if  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ , we have  $i \notin \pi([\widetilde{k}])$ , then by part 1 we know that  $\psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1) < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 2$ . by definition of  $\psi$  function (definition f.4), if  $\psi(x) < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 2$ , we have  $|x| < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}$ .

fact f.7 (characterization of  $k$  (line 7),  $v^{\mathrm{new}}$  (line 7) of updatev). in the  $j$ -th iteration,  $\forall i \in [n]$ , let  $y_i = \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1) + \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)$ . let  $\pi : [n] \to [n]$  be the sorting such that  $y_{\pi(i)} \geq y_{\pi(i+1)}$ . we have the following guarantees from the description of procedure softthreshold (algorithm 7).

1.  $\left\{i\in [n]:\psi (w_i^{(j + 1)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1) + \psi (w_i^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 /(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})\right\} \subseteq \pi ([k])$  
2.  $v_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} w_{i}^{(j + 1)}, & \text{if } i \in \pi([k]); \\ v_{i}^{(j)}, & \text{otherwise}. \end{array} \right.$  
3.  $\forall i\notin \pi ([k])$ $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} - 1| <   \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}.$

proof. part 1 and 2. from line 7 of updatev (algorithm 9)  $v^{\mathrm{new}} \in \mathbb{r}^n$  is the output of

$$
\mathrm {s o f t t h r e s h o l d} (y _ {i} \gets \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1), w ^ {(j + 1)}, v ^ {(j)}, \frac {\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}}{3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}, n ^ {a}).
$$

the statements then follow from a similar proof as that of part 1 and 2 of fact f.6.

part 3. from part 2,  $v_{i}^{\mathrm{new}}$  has two possible values. if  $v_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} = w_{i}^{(j + 1)}$ , we have  $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} - 1| = 0$ . if  $v_{i}^{\mathrm{new}} = v_{i}^{(j)}$ , we have  $i \notin \pi([k])$ , then by part 1 we know that  $\psi(w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)} - 1) < \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 / (32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}) < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 2$  (assumption f.5). then  $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)} - 1| < \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}$  by definition f.4.

fact f.8 ( $\widetilde{v}$  is far away from  $v$ ). for any  $j \in \{0, \dots, t\}$ , for any  $i \in [n]$ , either  $v_i^{(j)} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ , or  $|\widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1| > \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}$ .

proof. we prove it by induction. when initializing, we set  $v^{(0)} = \widetilde{v}^{(0)}$  (line 14 of initialize, algorithm 5), so the statement is true when  $j = 0$ .

in later iterations,  $v$  and  $\widetilde{v}$  can only be modified by procedure updatev. and in updatev (algorithm 9), the if branch on line 6 ensures that we can enter at most one of matrixupdate or partialmatrexupdate. now we analyze iteration  $j$  by looking into these two cases.

case 1. when we enter procedure matrixupdate, we always set  $v \gets v^{\text{new}}$  and  $\widetilde{v} \gets v^{\text{new}}$  (line 13 of algorithm 13). so  $v^{(j+1)} = \widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}$ , and the statement holds in this case.

case 2. when we enter procedure partialmatrexupdate, we know we did not enter procedure matrixupdate due to the else-if branch, and we do not modify  $v$  in partialmatrexupdate, so  $v$  does not change, i.e.,  $v^{(j + 1)} = v^{(j)}$ .

and for  $\widetilde{v}$ , we set  $\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)} \gets \widetilde{v}^{\text{new}}$  (line 16 in algorithm 14), where  $\widetilde{v}^{\text{new}}$  is defined on line 5 of procedure updatev(algorithm 9). by part 3 of fact f.6 we have

$$
\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \leftarrow \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {\text {n e w}} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} v _ {i} ^ {(j)}, & \text {i f} i \in \pi ([ \widetilde {k} ]) \text {a n d} w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \in [ (1 - \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)} ]; \\ w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)}, & \text {i f} i \in \pi ([ \widetilde {k} ]) \text {b u t} w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \notin [ (1 - \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)} ]; \\ \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}, & \text {o t h e r w i s e}. \end{array} \right.
$$

in the first case, we have  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j + 1)} = v_i^{(j)} = v_i^{(j + 1)}$ , and the lemma statement holds.

in the second case, we have  $\left|\widetilde{v}_i^{(j + 1)} / v_i^{(j + 1)} - 1\right| = \left|w_i^{(j + 1)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1\right| > \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}$ , and the lemma statement is also true.

in the third case we have  $\widetilde{v}^{(j + 1)} = \widetilde{v}^{(j)}$ . the lemma statement is true by induction hypothesis.

the following corollary follows from the above fact and triangle inequality:

corollary f.9. for any  $j \in \{0, \dots, t\}$  and any  $i \in [n]$ , if  $v_{i}^{(j)} \neq \widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}$ , then  $\forall x \in \mathbb{r}$ , we have

$$
| \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \geq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 2.
$$

proof. since  $v_{i}^{(j)} \neq \widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)}$ , from fact f.8, we have

$$
\left| \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 \right| > \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}}. \tag {47}
$$

we consider the following two cases depend on the value of  $\left|\widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)}\right|$ :

case 1,  $\left| \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)} \right| \leq 1$ : we have

$$
| \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | = | \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \cdot | \frac {v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \geq | \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \geq | \frac {\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | > \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}.
$$

where the second step follows from the assumption of case 1 that  $\left|\overline{v}_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)}\right| \leq 1$ , the third step follows from triangle inequality, and the fourth step follows from eq. (47).

case 2,  $\left| \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)} \right| > 1$ : we have

$$
| \frac {\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | = \frac {| (\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}) / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} |}{| \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} |} \geq \frac {| (\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}) / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} |}{| (\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}) / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} | + 1} \geq \frac {\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} + 1} \geq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 2.
$$

where the second step follows from triangle inequality, the third step follows from eq. (47) and the fact that function  $\frac{x}{x + 1}$  is monotonically increasing, and the last step follows from  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} \leq 1$ .

then similar as case 1 we have

$$
| \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | = | \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \cdot | \frac {\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \geq | \frac {x - \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | + | \frac {x - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | \geq | \frac {\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}} | > \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 2.
$$

fact f.10 (lower bound on  $k_j$ ). in the  $j$ -th iteration, we have that either  $k_j = 0$ , or  $k_j \geq n^a$ .

proof. recall that  $k_{j}$  is the second returned value by updatev on line 4 of updatequery (algorithm 8). if in updatev (algorithm 9) we do not enter the if-branch on line 6, then by the return clauses on line 19 and line 22, we have that  $k_{j} = 0$ .

now we only need to prove that when we enter the if-branch on line 6 of updatev (algo-rithm 9), the returned value  $k_{j} = k \geq n^{a}$ . when the if-clause on line 6 is true, we have

$$
\left| \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\widetilde {v} ^ {\text {n e w}} - v ^ {(j)}\right) \right| \geq n ^ {a}, \tag {48}
$$

where  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}}$  is defined on line 5.  $\forall i\in [n]$ , let  $y_{i} = \psi (w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)} - 1) + \psi (w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / \tilde{v}_{i}^{(j)} - 1)$ . let  $\pi :[n]\to [n]$  be the sorting such that  $y_{\pi (i)}\geq y_{\pi (i + 1)}$ . from part 1 of fact f.7, we have

$$
\left\{i \in [ n ]: \psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1\right) + \psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1\right) \geq \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}} ^ {2} / \left(3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \right\} \subseteq \pi ([ k ]). \tag {49}
$$

now it suffices to prove

$$
\operatorname {s u p p} \left(\widetilde {v} ^ {\text {n e w}} - v ^ {(j)}\right) \subseteq \left\{i: \psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1\right) + \psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1\right) \geq \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}} ^ {2} / \left(3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \right\}, \tag {50}
$$

because then we would have

$$
\begin{array}{l} k _ {j} = k = | \pi ([ k ]) | \geq \left| \left\{i: \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) \geq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2} / (3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}) \right\} \right| \\ \geq \left| \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\widetilde {v} ^ {\text {n e w}} - v ^ {(j)}\right) \right| \geq n ^ {a}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows from the definition of  $k_{j}$ , the third step follows from eq. (49), the fourth step follows from eq. (50), and the fifth step follows from eq. (48).

now it remains to prove eq. (50). we first prove that

$$
\operatorname {s u p p} \left(\widetilde {v} ^ {\text {n e w}} - v ^ {(j)}\right) \subseteq \left\{i: \left| w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 \right| + \left| w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 \right| \geq \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}} / 2 \right\}.
$$

using part 3 of fact f.6 we know that  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  can be  $v_{i}^{(j)}$ ,  $w_{i}^{(j + 1)}$ , or  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ . so for any  $i \in \operatorname{supp}(\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}} - v^{(j)})$ , since  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} \neq v_i^{(j)}$ , we know that  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}}$  is either  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$  or  $w_{i}^{(j + 1)}$ . we consider these two cases:

1.  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}\neq v_i^{(j)}$  using corollary f.9, and plugging in  $x\gets w_i^{(j + 1)}$  we directly have that  $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)} - 1| + |w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_{i}^{(j)} - 1|\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} / 2.$  
2.  $\widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = w_i^{(j + 1)}\neq v_i^{(j)}$  . by part 3 of fact f.6 we have  $w_{i}^{(j + 1)}\notin [(1 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v_{i}^{(j)},(1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v_{i}^{(j)}],$  which then gives us  $|w_i^{(j + 1)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1|\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} / 2.$

thus we always have that  $\forall i\in \mathrm{supp}(\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}} - v^{(j)})$ $|w_i^{(j + 1)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1| + |w_i^{(j + 1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1|\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} / 2.$  so at least one of the following is true:

$$
| w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 | \geq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 4 \quad \mathrm {o r} \quad | w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} - 1 | \geq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 4.
$$

without loss of generality, assume  $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)} - 1| \geq \frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}}{4}$ . we have

$$
\psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) \geq \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) \geq \psi (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 4) = \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2} / (3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}).
$$

where the first two steps follow from  $\psi$  is non-negative and non-decreasing, and the third step follows from  $\psi (\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} / 4) = \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 /(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$  (see definition f.4 of  $\psi$ ). thus this proves eq. (50).

fact f.11 (lower bound on  $\widetilde{k}_j$ ). in the  $j$ -th iteration, we have that either  $\widetilde{k}_j = 0$ , or  $\widetilde{k}_j \geq n^{\widetilde{a}}$ .

proof. recall that  $\widetilde{k}_j$  is the third returned value by updatev on line 4 of updatequery (algorithm 8). if in updatev (algorithm 9) we do not enter the else-if branch on line 14, then by the return clauses on line 12 and line 22, we have that  $\widetilde{k}_j = 0$ .

now we only need to prove that when we enter the else-if branch on line 14 of updatev (algorithm 9), the returned value  $\widetilde{k}_j = \widetilde{k} \geq n^{\widetilde{a}}$ . when the if-clause on line 14 is true, we have

$$
\left| \operatorname {s u p p} \left(\widetilde {v} ^ {\text {n e w}} - \widetilde {v} ^ {(j)}\right) \right| \geq n ^ {\widetilde {a}}.
$$

from part 3 of fact f.6, we have  $|\operatorname{supp}(\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}} - \widetilde{v}^{(j)})| \subseteq \pi([\widetilde{k}])$ . therefore,

$$
\widetilde {k} _ {j} = \widetilde {k} \geq | \operatorname {s u p p} (\widetilde {v} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} - \widetilde {v} ^ {(j)}) | \geq n ^ {\widetilde {a}}.
$$

fact f.12 (characterization of matrixupdate). assume assumption f.5 is true. in the  $j$ -th iteration,  $\forall i \in [n]$ , let  $y_i = \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / v_i^{(j)} - 1) + \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)$ . let  $\pi : [n] \to [n]$  be the sorting such that  $y_{\pi(i)} \geq y_{\pi(i+1)}$ . if  $k_j > 0$ , we have the following:

1.  $k_{j}$  satisfies that either  $k_{j} = n$  or  $y_{\pi(k_{j})} < (1 - 1 / \log n) \cdot y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}$ .  
2.  $y_{\pi (k_j)}\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 /(3200\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$  
3. after the procedure matrixupdate,

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)}, & \forall i \leq k _ {j}; \\ \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} = \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)}, & \forall i > k _ {j}. \end{array} \right.
$$

4. the running time of matrixupdate in the  $j$ th iteration is  $\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(k_j,n^{1 + o(1)},n)$ .

proof. part 1 and part 2. note that as long as  $k_{j} > 0$ ,  $k_{j}$  is the  $k$  computed in line 7 in procedure updatev(algorithm 9). by fact f.10 and  $k_{j} \neq 0$ , we have  $k_{j} \geq n^{a}$ . therefore, when calculating  $k$  using one call of softthreshold (line 7 in algorithm 9), we must have entered the repeat-until branch (line 7 to 9 in algorithm 7). so  $k_{j}$  must satisfy the end condition of the repeat-loop that either  $k_{j} = n$  or  $y_{\pi(k_{j})} < (1 - 1 / \log n) \cdot y_{\pi(k_{j}/1.5)}$ . this finishes the proof of part 1.

further, let  $k^*$  denote the largest index such that  $y_{\pi (k^{*})}\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2} / (32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$ . we have

$$
y _ {\pi (k _ {j})} \geq (1 - 1 / \log n) ^ {\log_ {1. 5} k _ {j} - \log_ {1. 5} k ^ {*}} \cdot y _ {\pi (k ^ {*})} \geq (1 - 1 / \log n) ^ {\log_ {1. 5} n} \cdot \frac {\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}}{3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}} \geq \frac {\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}}{3 2 0 0 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}},
$$

where the first step follows from the repeat-loop (line 8 in algorithm 7), the second step follows from  $\log_{1.5}k_j - \log_{1.5}k^*\leq \log_{1.5}n$  and  $y_{\pi (k^{*})}\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^{2} / (32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$ , and the last step follows from the fact that for  $n\geq 4$ ,  $(1 - 1 / \log n)^{\log_{1.5}n}\geq 1 / 100$ . this finishes the proof of part 2.

part 3. from line 13 of matrixupdate (algorithm 13) we have that  $v^{(j + 1)} = \widetilde{v}^{(j + 1)} = v^{\mathrm{new}}$ . using part 2 of fact f.7, we have that

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l l} v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {\text {n e w}} = w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)}, & \forall i \leq k _ {j}; \\ v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {\text {n e w}} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)}, & \forall i > k _ {j}, \end{array} \right. \tag {51}
$$

so we have

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)}, & \forall i \leq k _ {j}; \\ \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} = v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)}, & \forall i > k _ {j}. \end{array} \right.
$$

note that by part 1, either  $k_{j} = n$  or  $y_{\pi (k_j)} < (1 - 1 / \log n)\cdot y_{\pi (k_j / 1.5)}$ . if  $k_{j} = n$ , there is no  $i > k_{j}$ , so the proof is already finished.

otherwise, for any  $i > k_{j}$ , we prove that  $\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} = v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}$  by contradiction. if  $\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} \neq v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}$ , using corollary f.9 and plugging in  $x \gets w_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)}$ , we have  $|w_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)} / v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} - 1| + |w_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} - 1| \geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} / 2$ , so at least one of the following is true:

$$
\left| w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1 \right| \geq \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}} / 4 \quad \text {o r} \quad \left| w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1 \right| \geq \epsilon_ {\text {f a r}} / 4.
$$

thus we have

$$
y _ {\pi (i)} = \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) \geq \psi (\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} / 4) = \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2} / (3 2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}),
$$

where the first two steps follow from  $\psi$  is non-negative and non-decreasing, and the third step follows from  $\psi (\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} / 4) = \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 /(32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$  (see definition f.4 of  $\psi$ ).

then we have  $y_{\pi(k_j)} \geq y_{\pi(i)} \geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 / (32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$  since  $y$  is decreasingly sorted according to  $\pi$  and  $i > k_j$ . but from the initial assignment of  $k_j$  on line 5 of softthreshold(algorithm 7), and that  $k_j$  can only strictly increase afterwards, we have that  $y_{\pi(k_j)} < \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 / (32\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$ . this leads to contradiction. thus we have  $\widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)} = v_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)} = v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} = \widetilde{v}_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}, \forall i > k_j$ .

part 4. this directly follows from lemma e.12 which proves the running time of matrixupdate per call.

fact f.13 (characterization of partialmatrixupdate). assume assumption f.5 is true. in the  $j$ -th iteration,  $\forall i \in [n]$ , let  $y_i = \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)$ . let  $\pi : [n] \to [n]$  be the sorting such that  $y_{\pi(i)} \geq y_{\pi(i+1)}$ . if  $\widetilde{k}_j > 0$ , we have the following:

1.  $\widetilde{k}_j$  satisfies that either  $\widetilde{k}_j = n$  or  $y_{\pi (\widetilde{k}_j)} < (1 - 1 / \log n)\cdot y_{\pi (\widetilde{k}_j / 1.5)}$ .  
2.  $y_{\pi (\widetilde{k}_j)}\geq \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / 100$  
3. after the procedure partialmatrixupdate,  $\forall i\in \pi ([\widetilde{k}_j])$ $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j + 1)}$  satisfies

$$
\psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - 1\right) \leq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} / (2 0 0 \log n), \tag {52}
$$

and  $\forall i \notin \pi([\widetilde{k}_j])$ ,  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ . also,  $\forall i \in [n]$ ,  $v_i^{(j+1)} = v_i^{(j)}$ .

4. the running time of partialmatrixupdate in the  $j$ -th iteration is  $\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(\widetilde{k}_j,n^a,n^a)$ .

proof. part 1 and 2. note that as long as  $\widetilde{k}_j > 0$ ,  $\widetilde{k}_j$  is the  $\widetilde{k}$  computed in line 4 in procedure updatev (algorithm 9). by fact f.11, we have  $\widetilde{k} \geq n^{\widetilde{a}}$ . so by a similar argument as that of the proof part 1 and 2 of fact f.12, we can prove part 1 and 2 of this fact.

part 3. because we do not modify  $v$  in partialmatrixupdate, so  $\forall i \in [n]$ ,  $v_i^{(j+1)} = v_i^{(j)}$ .

in procedure partialmatrixupdate, we set  $\widetilde{v}^{(j + 1)}\gets \widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}}$  (line 16 of algorithm 14), where  $\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{new}}$  is created by one call to softthreshold (line 4 in updatev, algorithm 8). by part 3 of fact f.6, we have

$$
\widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {\mathrm {n e w}} \leftarrow \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} v _ {i} ^ {(j)}, & \text {i f} i \in \pi ([ \widetilde {k} ]) \text {a n d} w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \in [ (1 - \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)} ]; \\ w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)}, & \text {i f} i \in \pi ([ \widetilde {k} ]) \text {b u t} w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \notin [ (1 - \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)}, (1 + \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}) v _ {i} ^ {(j)} ]; \\ \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)}, & \text {o t h e r w i s e .} \end{array} \right.
$$

for  $i \notin \pi([\widetilde{k}_j])$ ,  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ .

for  $i \in \pi([\widetilde{k}_j])$ , if  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} = \widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = w_i^{(j+1)}$ , eq. (52) is trivially true since  $\psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} - 1) = 0$ .

otherwise  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j + 1)} = \widetilde{v}_i^{\mathrm{new}} = v_i^{(j)}$  and  $w_{i}^{(j + 1)}\in [(1 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v_{i}^{(j)},(1 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{far}})v_{i}^{(j)}]$ , we have:

$$
\psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - 1\right) = \psi \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1\right) \leq \psi \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}}\right) = \epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2} / \left(2 \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \leq \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} / (2 0 0 \log n)\right),
$$

where the last step is by  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}} \leq \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} / (100 \log n)$  (assumption f.5).

part 4. this directly follows from lemma e.18 which proves the running time of partialma-trixupdate per call.

corollary f.14. assume assumption f.5 is true. in the  $j$ -th iteration, if we enter partialma-trixupdate, we must have  $\forall i \in [n]$ ,  $\psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} - 1) \leq \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)$ .

proof. if we enter partialmatrixupdate, we must have  $\widetilde{k}_j > 0$ . by part 2,3 of fact f.13, there is some permutation  $\pi$  such that  $\forall i \in \pi([\widetilde{k}_j])$ ,

$$
\psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) \geq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} / 1 0 0 \quad \mathrm {a n d} \quad \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - 1) \leq \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} / (2 0 0 \log n) <   \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} / 1 0 0.
$$

and also by part 3 of fact f.13,  $\forall i \notin \pi([\widetilde{k}_j])$ ,  $\widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} = \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)}$ . therefore,  $\forall i \notin \pi([\widetilde{k}_j])$ , we have that  $\psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j+1)} - 1) = \psi(w_i^{(j+1)} / \widetilde{v}_i^{(j)} - 1)$ .

# f.3 amortized analysis for matrixupdate

# f.3.1 definitions

definition f.15 (  $x$  and  $y$  for matrixupdate). for any  $j\in \{0,1,\ldots ,t - 1\}$  , and for any  $i\in [n]$  we define  $x_{i}^{(j)}$  and  $y_{i}^{(j)}$  as follows:

$$
x _ {i} ^ {(j)} := \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1), \quad y _ {i} ^ {(j)} := \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1),
$$

where  $v^{(j)}$ ,  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$  and  $w^{(j)}$  are defined as of definition f.1.

note that the difference between  $x_{i}^{(j)}$  and  $y_{i}^{(j)}$  is that  $w$  changes from  $w^{(j)}$  to  $w^{(j + 1)}$ . the difference between  $y_{i}^{(j)}$  and  $x_{i}^{(j + 1)}$  is that  $v$  and  $\widetilde{v}$  changes from  $v^{(j)}, \widetilde{v}^{(j)}$  to  $v^{(j + 1)}, \widetilde{v}^{(j + 1)}$ .

for convenience, we define permutations of the coordinates that are sorted according to  $x$  or  $y$ .

definition f.16 (sorting permutations for matrixupdate). for any  $j \in \{0, 1, \ldots, t\}$ , let  $\tau_j$  be the permutation such that  $x_{\tau_j(i)}^{(j)} \geq x_{\tau_j(i + 1)}^{(j)}$ , and let  $\pi_j$  be the permutation such that  $y_{\pi_j(i)}^{(j)} \geq y_{\pi_j(i + 1)}^{(j)}$ .

when it is clear from the context that we are only arguing about the  $j$ -th iteration, for simplicity we assume the coordinates of vector  $x^{(j)} \in \mathbb{r}^n$  are sorted such that  $x_i^{(j)} \geq x_{i+1}^{(j)}$ . and we use  $\tau$  and  $\pi$  to denote the permutations such that  $x_{\tau(i)}^{(j+1)} \geq x_{\tau(i+1)}^{(j+1)}$  and  $y_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} \geq y_{\pi(i+1)}^{(j)}$ .

definition f.17 (  $g$  for matrixupdate). for some  $a \leq \alpha$ , where  $\alpha$  is the dual exponent of matrix multiplication, we define  $g \in \mathbb{r}^n$  as follows:

$$
g _ {i} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} n ^ {- a}, & \text {i f} i \leq n ^ {a}; \\ i ^ {\frac {\omega - 2}{1 - a} - 1} \cdot n ^ {- \frac {a (\omega - 2)}{1 - a}}, & \text {i f} i \in (n ^ {a}, n ]. \end{array} \right.
$$

note that  $g$  is non-increasing. for all  $k_{j} \in (n^{a}, n]$ ,  $(k_{j} \cdot g_{k_{j}} n^{2}) = k_{j}^{\frac{\omega - 2}{1 - a}} \cdot n^{2 - \frac{a(\omega - 2)}{1 - a}}$  is an upper bound of the running time  $\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n, n, k_{j})$  of multiplying a  $n \times n$  matrix with a  $n \times k_{j}$  matrix. for more details please refer to [gu18].

definition f.18 (potential function  $\phi$  for matrixupdate). we define the potential function in the  $j$ -th iteration as

$$
\phi_ {j} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot x _ {\tau_ {j} (i)} ^ {(j)}.
$$

note that we always have  $\phi_j \geq 0$  since  $\forall i$ ,  $g_i$  and  $x_i^{(j)}$  are both non-negative.

# f.3.2 main result

lemma f.19 (amortized time for matrixupdate). let sequences  $\{w^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^t$ ,  $\{v^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^t$ ,  $\{\widetilde{v}^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^t$  be defined as of definition f.1, let  $k_{j}$  be defined as of definition f.3, and let  $\{x^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^t$ ,  $\{y^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^t$ ,  $g$ ,  $\phi$  be defined as of definition f.15, f.17 and f.18. if we further have the condition that the input sequence satisfies the following:  $\forall j \in \{0, \dots, t - 1\}$

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\mathbb {e} [ w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} | w ^ {(j)} ] / w _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) ^ {2} \leq c _ {1} ^ {2}, \quad \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} (\mathbb {e} [ (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / w _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) ^ {2} | w ^ {(j)} ]) ^ {2} \leq c _ {2} ^ {2}, \quad | w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / w _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 | \leq 1 / 4.
$$

then, we have that in expectation

$$
\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {t} k _ {j} g _ {k _ {j}} = o \bigl (\bigl (\frac {c _ {1} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} \bigr) \cdot \log n \cdot \| g \| _ {2} \bigr).
$$

further, combining with lemma e.12, the expected amortized running time per iteration of matrixupdate is

$$
o \big ((\frac {c _ {1} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}}) \cdot (n ^ {2 - a / 2} + n ^ {\omega - 1 / 2}) \log n \big).
$$

proof. first note that in the  $j$ -th iteration, the value of the potential  $\phi_j$  depends on  $w^{(j)}$ ,  $v^{(j)}$  and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$ . and the value of  $v^{(j)}$  and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$  are affected by both matrixupdate and partialmatrixupdate. we upper bound how much the potential function can increase due to changing  $w^{(j)}$  to  $w^{(j+1)}$  in section f.3.3, and we also lower bound how much the potential function can decrease because of changing  $v^{(j)}$  to  $v^{(j+1)}$  and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$  to  $\widetilde{v}^{(j+1)}$  in section f.3.4.

in the beginning  $v^{(0)} = \widetilde{v}^{(0)} = w^{(0)}$ , so  $\phi_0 = 0$ . also note that  $\phi_j \geq 0, \forall j \in [t]$ . thus we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 0 \leq \mathbb {e} [ \phi_ {t} ] - \phi_ {0} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {t - 1} \mathbb {e} [ \phi_ {j + 1} - \phi_ {j} ] = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {t - 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ x _ {\tau (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} - x _ {i} ^ {(j)} \right] \\ = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \underbrace {\mathbb {e} \left[ y _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - x _ {i} ^ {(j)} \right]} _ {w \text {m o v e}} - \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \underbrace {\mathbb {e} \left[ y _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - x _ {\tau (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} \right]} _ {v, \widetilde {v} \text {m o v e}}\right) \\ \leq \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {t - 1} \left(o \left(c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \cdot \| g \| _ {2} - \omega \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2} \cdot k _ {j} \cdot g _ {k _ {j}} / \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} \log n\right)\right)\right) \\ = t \cdot o \left(c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \| g \| _ {2} - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {t} \omega \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2} \cdot k _ {j} \cdot g _ {k _ {j}} / \left(\epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} \log n\right)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second step follows from splitting terms and the fact that  $\phi_0$  is deterministic, the third step follows from the definition of  $\phi$  (definition f.18), the fourth step follows from splitting terms, the fifth step follows from lemma f.20 which states that  $\forall w^{(j)}, v^{(j)}, \widetilde{v}^{(j)}$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ y _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - x _ {i} ^ {(j)} \mid w ^ {(j)}, v ^ {(j)}, \widetilde {v} ^ {(j)} \right] \leq o \bigl (c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}} \bigr) \cdot \| g \| _ {2},
$$

then this upper bound also holds for unconditional expectation, the fifth step also follows from lemma f.24 which states that  $\sum_{i=1}^{n} g_i \cdot (y_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} - x_{\tau(i)}^{(j+1)}) \geq \omega (\epsilon_{\mathrm{far}}^2 \cdot k_j \cdot g_{k_j} / (\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} \log n))$ .

therefore, we have

$$
\frac {1}{t} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {t} k _ {j} g _ {k _ {j}} \leq o \big (\big (\frac {c _ {1} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} \big) \cdot \log n \cdot \| g \| _ {2} \big).
$$

using lemma e.12, we have that the expected amortized running time per iteration of ma-trixupdate is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{t} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {t} \mathcal {t} _ {\mathrm {m a t}} (n, n, k _ {j}) \leq \frac {n ^ {2}}{t} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {t} k _ {j} g _ {k _ {j}} = o \left(\left(\frac {c _ {1} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}}\right) \cdot n ^ {2} \log n \cdot \| g \| _ {2}\right) \\ = o \big ((\frac {c _ {1} \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}} + \frac {c _ {2}}{\epsilon_ {\mathrm {f a r}} ^ {2}}) \cdot (n ^ {2 - a / 2} + n ^ {\omega - 1 / 2}) \log n \big), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows from the definition of  $g$  which gives that  $\mathcal{t}_{\mathrm{mat}}(n,n,k_j)\leq n^2 k_jg_{k_j}$ , and the third step follows from lemma f.25 that  $\| g\| _2 = o(n^{-a / 2} + n^{\omega -5 / 2})$ .

# f.3.3  $w$  move

the goal of this section is to prove lemma f.20.

lemma f.20 (w move). in the  $j$ -th iteration, for any possible values  $w^{(j)}$ ,  $v^{(j)}$ , and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ y _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - x _ {i} ^ {(j)} \mid w ^ {(j)}, v ^ {(j)}, \widetilde {v} ^ {(j)} \right] \leq o \left(c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \| g \| _ {2}. \tag {53}
$$

proof. for simplicity, in this proof we write  $\mathbb{e}[\cdot ]$  as a shorthand of  $\mathbb{e}[\cdot |w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}]$ .

observe that since the non-negative values  $x_{i}^{(j)}$  are sorted in descending order, and  $g$  is also non-increasing, we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} x _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} x _ {i} ^ {(j)}. \tag {54}
$$

we then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ y _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - x _ {i} ^ {(j)} \right] \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ y _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - x _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} \right] \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) ] - \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) + \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} / \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) ] \\ = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) - \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) ] + \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / \tilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) - \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} / \tilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) ] \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows from eq.(54), the second step follows from the definitions of  $x^{(j)}$  and  $y^{(j)}$  (definition f.15).

now  $\sum_{i=1}^{n} g_i \cdot \mathbb{e}[y_{\pi(i)}^{(j)} - x_i^{(j)}] \leq o(c_1 + c_2 / \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}) \|g\|_2$  directly follows from lemma f.21 and lemma f.22.

it remains to prove the following two lemmas.

lemma f.21. under assumption f.5, in the  $j$ -th iteration, for any  $w^{(j)}$ ,  $v^{(j)}$ , and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$  we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) - \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} / v _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) | w ^ {(j)}, v ^ {(j)}, \widetilde {v} ^ {(j)} ] = o (c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}) \cdot \| g \| _ {2}.
$$

proof. for simplicity, in this proof we write  $\mathbb{e}[\cdot ]$  as a shorthand of  $\mathbb{e}[\cdot |w^{(j)},v^{(j)},\widetilde{v}^{(j)}]$ . and we also define  $x$  and  $y$  as

$$
y _ {i} = w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1, \quad x _ {i} = w _ {i} ^ {(j)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1, \tag {55}
$$

and they are only used in this proof. then the lemma statement becomes

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {\pi (i)}) - \psi (x _ {\pi (i)}) ] = o (c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}) \cdot \| g \| _ {2}.
$$

let  $i$  be the set of indices such that  $|x_{i}|\leq 1$ . we separate the term into two:

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {\pi (i)}) - \psi (x _ {\pi (i)}) ] = \sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) ] + \sum_ {i \in i ^ {c}} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) ].
$$

case 1: terms from  $i$ . mean value theorem shows that for any  $y_{i}$ , there exist  $\zeta$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) = \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) (y _ {i} - x _ {i}) + \frac {1}{2} \psi^ {\prime \prime} (\zeta) (y _ {i} - x _ {i}) ^ {2} \\ \leq \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) \cdot \frac {w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} + \frac {l _ {2}}{2} \cdot (\frac {w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}) ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second step follows from plugging in the definition of  $x_{i}$  and  $y_{i}$  in eq. (55), and letting  $l_{2} = \max_{x}\psi^{\prime \prime}(x)$ . taking conditional expectation (over  $w^{(j)}$ ,  $v^{(j)}$ , and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$ ) on both sides, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) ] \leq \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) \cdot \frac {\mathbb {e} [ w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} ] - w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} + \frac {l _ {2}}{2} \frac {1}{(v _ {i} ^ {(j)}) ^ {2}} \mathbb {e} [ (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(j)}) ^ {2} ] \\ = \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) \cdot \frac {w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} \cdot \beta_ {i} + \frac {l _ {2}}{2} \frac {(w _ {i} ^ {(j)}) ^ {2}}{(v _ {i} ^ {(j)}) ^ {2}} \gamma_ {i}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\beta_{i}$  and  $\gamma_{i}$  are defined as  $\beta_{i} = \mathbb{e}[w_{i}^{(j + 1)}] / w_{i}^{(j)} - 1$ ,  $\gamma_{i} = \mathbb{e}[(w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / w_{i}^{(j)} - 1)^{2}]$ .

this then gives us

$$
\sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) ] \leq \sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) \cdot \frac {w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} \cdot \beta_ {i} + \sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \frac {l _ {2}}{2} \cdot \frac {\left(w _ {i} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {2}}{\left(v _ {i} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {2}} \cdot \gamma_ {i}. \tag {56}
$$

for the term  $w_{i}^{(j)} / v_{i}^{(j)}$ , we note that for  $i \in i$ , we have

$$
\left| w _ {i} ^ {(j)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} \right| = \left| x _ {i} + 1 \right| \leq \left| x _ {i} \right| + 1 \leq 2, \tag {57}
$$

where the first step follows the definition of  $x_{i}$  in eq. (55), the second step follows from triangle inequality, and the third step follows from  $|x_{i}| \leq 1$  for  $i \in i$ .

using this, we can bound the first term of eq. (56) by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) \cdot \frac {w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}} \cdot \beta_ {i} \leq \left(\sum_ {i \in i} \left(g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \psi^ {\prime} (x _ {i}) \cdot \frac {w _ {i} ^ {(j)}}{v _ {i} ^ {(j)}}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \sum_ {i \in i} \beta_ {i} ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq \left(\sum_ {i \in i} \left(2 l _ {1} \cdot g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \sum_ {i \in i} \beta_ {i} ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq o \left(l _ {1}\right) \cdot \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} ^ {2} \cdot c _ {1} ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} = o \left(c _ {1} l _ {1} \| g \| _ {2}\right), \tag {58} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $l_{1} = \max_{x}|\psi '(x)|$ , the first step follows by cauchy-schwarz inequality, and the second step follows by  $|\psi^{\prime}(x_i)|\leq l_1$  and  $|w_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)}|\leq 2$  by eq.(57), and the third step follows from  $\sum_{i = 1}^{n}\beta_{i}^{2}\leq c_{1}^{2}$  from the lemma statement of lemma f.19.

for the second term of eq. (56), we have

$$
\sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \frac {l _ {2}}{2} \cdot \frac {\left(w _ {i} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {2}}{\left(v _ {i} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {2}} \cdot \gamma_ {i} \leq o (l _ {2}) \cdot \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \gamma_ {i} \leq o (l _ {2}) \cdot \| g \| _ {2} \cdot \| \gamma \| _ {2} = o \left(c _ {2} l _ {2} \| g \| _ {2}\right), \tag {59}
$$

where the first step follows from  $|w_i^{(j)} / v_i^{(j)}| \leq 2$  by eq.(57), the second step follows from cauchy-schwarz inequality, and the third step follows from  $\sum_{i=1}^{n} \gamma_i^2 \leq c_2^2$  from the lemma statement of lemma f.19.

now, plugging the bound of eq. (58) and eq. (59) into eq. (56) and using that  $l_{1} = o(1)$ ,  $l_{2} = o(1 / \epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}})$  (from part 4 of lemma f.37), we have that

$$
\sum_ {i \in i} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) ] \leq o \left(c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}\right) \cdot \| g \| _ {2}.
$$

case 2: terms from  $i^c$ . for all  $i \in i^c$ , we have  $|x_i| > 1$ . note that  $\psi(x)$  is a constant for  $x \geq 2\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}}$ , and from assumption f.5 we assume that  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} \leq 1/4$ . therefore, if  $|y_i| \geq 1/2$ , we have that  $\psi(y_i) - \psi(x_i) = 0$ . hence, we only need to consider the  $i \in i^c$  such that  $|y_i| < 1/2$ . for these  $i$ , we have that

$$
\left| y _ {i} - x _ {i} \right| \geq \left| x _ {i} \right| - \left| y _ {i} \right| > 1 / 2, \tag {60}
$$

where the first step follows by triangle inequality, and the second step follows from the assumptions  $|x_i| > 1$  and  $|y_i| < 1/2$ . we also have

$$
\left| y _ {i} - x _ {i} \right| = \left| \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(j)}\right) / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} \right| = \left| w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {i} ^ {(j)} \right| \cdot \left| \left(w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(j)}\right) / w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \right| \leq \frac {3}{2} \left| 1 - w _ {i} ^ {(j)} / w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} \right|, \tag {61}
$$

where the first step follows from the definition of  $y_{i}$  and  $x_{i}$  in eq. (55), the third step follows from  $|y_{i}| = |w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)} - 1| < 1 / 2$  and thus  $|w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / v_{i}^{(j)}| < 3 / 2$ .

combining eq. (60) and eq. (61), we have that  $\left| 1 - w_{i}^{(j)} / w_{i}^{(j + 1)} \right| > 1 / 3$  and hence  $\left| w_{i}^{(j)} / w_{i}^{(j + 1)} \right| < 2 / 3$  or  $\left| w_{i}^{(j)} / w_{i}^{(j + 1)} \right| > 4 / 3$ , which then gives us  $\left| w_{i}^{(j + 1)} / w_{i}^{(j)} - 1 \right| > 1 / 4$ , but this contradicts with the lemma statement of lemma f.19, so  $|y_{i}| < 1 / 2$  is impossible.

hence, we have

$$
\sum_ {i \in i ^ {c}} g _ {\pi^ {- 1} (i)} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \psi (y _ {i}) - \psi (x _ {i}) ] = 0.
$$

combining both cases, we have the result.

lemma f.22. in the  $j$ -th iteration, for any  $w^{(j)}$ ,  $v^{(j)}$ , and  $\widetilde{v}^{(j)}$  we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) - \psi (w _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} / \widetilde {v} _ {\pi (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) \big | w ^ {(j)}, v ^ {(j)}, \widetilde {v} ^ {(j)} \right] = o (c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}) \cdot \| g \| _ {2}.
$$

proof. the proof of this lemma is exactly the same as that of lemma f.21, just replace all  $v$  with  $\widetilde{v}$  in the proof of lemma f.21.

note that in the proof of lemma f.21 we do not have any requirement on  $v$ , so in fact we have the following more generalized lemma.

lemma f.23 (generalized "w move" lemma). let  $\{w^{(j)}\}_{j = 0}^{t}$  be a random sequence that satisfies  $\forall j\in \{0,\dots,t - 1\}$ ,

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \big (\mathbb {e} [ w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} | w ^ {(j)} ] / w _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 \big) ^ {2} \leq c _ {1} ^ {2}, \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \big (\mathbb {e} [ (w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / w _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1) ^ {2} | w ^ {(j)} ] \big) ^ {2} \leq c _ {2} ^ {2}, | w _ {i} ^ {(j + 1)} / w _ {i} ^ {(j)} - 1 | \leq 1 / 4.
$$

let  $\{v^{(j)}\}_{j=0}^t$  be another random sequence such that  $\forall j \in [t]$ ,  $v^{(j)}$  only depends on  $w^{(j)}$  and  $v^{(j-1)}$ . let  $\psi$  be defined as of definition f.4, where the parameter  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{mp}} < 1/4$ . let  $g \in \mathbb{r}^n$  be a sequence that is non-increasing, i.e.,  $g_1 \geq g_2 \geq \dots \geq g_n$ . let  $\pi_j : [n] \to [n]$  be the sorting  $\psi(w_{\pi(i)}^{(j+1)} / v_{\pi(i)}^{(j)}) - 1 \geq \psi(w_{\pi(i+1)}^{(j+1)} / v_{\pi(i+1)}^{(j)}) - 1$ .

then  $\forall j\in \{0,\dots,t - 1\}$ , in the  $j$ -th iteration, for any  $w^{(j)}$  and  $v^{(j)}$  we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} g _ {i} \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \psi (w _ {\pi_ {j} (i)} ^ {(j + 1)} / v _ {\pi_ {j} (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) - \psi (w _ {\pi_ {j} (i)} ^ {(j)} / v _ {\pi_ {j} (i)} ^ {(j)} - 1) \big | w ^ {(j)}, v ^ {(j)} \right] = o (c _ {1} + c _ {2} / \epsilon_ {\mathrm {m p}}) \cdot \| g \| _ {2}.
$$
